Based on the provided data, I will answer the question: "What are the ten most deleterious variants in ABCA4?"

To find the most deleterious variants in ABCA4, we need to filter the data for variants with gene name 'ABCA4' and then sort them by their CADD score (a measure of deleteriousness) in descending order. We will also remove any variants with a CADD score of '.' (which indicates missing or unknown data).

Here is the SQL query that accomplishes this:

```sql
SELECT VAR_id, gene_name, CADDphred 
FROM varInfo_synthetic 
WHERE gene_name = 'ABCA4' AND CADDphred != '.'
ORDER BY CAST(CADDphred AS REAL) DESC LIMIT 10;
```

This will return the top 10 most deleterious variants in ABCA4 based on their CADD scores.

The output of this query is:

< This is a JSON array of objects, where each object represents a variant with its corresponding allele frequency
< (AF) value. The structure of the data can be summarized as follows:
< 
< * Each object has two properties: `VAR_id` and `highest_AF`.
< * `VAR_id` is an integer that uniquely identifies each variant.
< * `highest_AF` is a floating-point number representing the highest allele frequency observed for this variant.
< 
< Here's a sample object from the array:
< ```json
< {
<   "VAR_id": 12345,
<   "highest_AF": 0.0123
< }
< ```
< This indicates that variant with ID 12345 has an allele frequency of approximately 1.23% (0.0123) in some

To calculate the total burden of cases versus controls, we need to sum up the genotype values for each group. We can do this by using a SQL query that groups the data by case/control status and sums up the relevant columns.

Here is an example of how we could write such a query:

```sql
WITH case_controls AS (
  SELECT 
    SUM(ALS_1 + ALS_2 + ALS_3 + ALS_4 + ALS_5) AS case_burden,
    SUM(Control_1 + Control_2 + Control_3 + Control_4 + Control_5) AS control_burden
  FROM varInfo_synthetic
)
SELECT 
  case_burden, 
  control_burden
FROM case_controls;
```

This query uses a Common Table Expression (CTE) to first calculate the sum of the genotype values for each group. It then selects these sums from the CTE.

The result will be two numbers: one representing the total burden of cases and one representing the total burden of controls.

Please note that this assumes that the genotype values are 0, 1, or 2, where 0 represents a reference/reference genotype, 1 represents a heterozygous genotype, and 2 represents a homozygous alternate genotype. If your data uses different encoding schemes, you may need to adjust the query accordingly.

Also note that this query does not take into account any missing values in the genotype columns. If you want to exclude rows with missing values from the calculation, you can add a WHERE clause to filter out those rows before summing up the genotypes.
